A minimal set of generators for the polynomial algebra of five variables in a generic degree
Nguyễn Sum, Phạm Đỗ Tài

Abstract
. Let P k P_{k} be the graded polynomial algebra 𝔽 2 ​ [ x 1 , x 2 , … , x k ] \mathbb{F}_{2}[x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{k}] over the prime field with two elements, 𝔽 2 \mathbb{F}_{2} , with the degree of each x i x_{i} being 1. We study the hit problem , set up by Frank Peterson, of finding a minimal set of generators for P k P_{k} as a module over the mod- 2 2 Steenrod algebra, 𝒜 . \mathcal{A}. It is an open problem in Algebraic Topology. In this paper, we explicitly determine a minimal set of 𝒜 \mathcal{A} -generators for P 5 P_{5} in the case of the generic degree m = 2 d m=2^{d} for all d ⩾ 8 d\geqslant 8 .

1
1
footnotetext:
2000
Mathematics Subject Classification
. Primary 55S10; 55S05.
2
2
footnotetext:
Keywords and phrases:
Steenrod squares, Peterson hit problem, polynomial algebra, modular representation.

1.
Introduction

Let
V_{k}
be an elementary abelian 2-group of rank
k
and let
BV_{k}
be the classifying space of
V_{k}
. Then,

P_{k}:=H^{*}(BV_{k})\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{k}],

a polynomial algebra in
k
variables
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{k}
, each of degree 1. Here, the cohomology is taken with coefficients in the prime field
\mathbb{F}_{2}
with two elements. The algebra
P_{k}
is a module over the mod-2 Steenrod algebra,
\mathcal{A}
. The action of
\mathcal{A}
on
P_{k}
is determined by the formula

Sq^{t}(x_{i})=\begin{cases}x_{i},&t=0,\\
x_{i}^{2},&t=1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

and subject to the Cartan formula

Sq^{m}(uv)=\sum_{i=0}^{m}Sq^{i}(u)Sq^{m-i}(v),

for
u,\,v\in P_{k}
(see Steenrod and Epstein
[
19
]
).

A polynomial
g
in
P_{k}
is called
hit
if it can be written as a finite sum
g=\sum_{i\geqslant 0}Sq^{i}(\ell_{i})
for some polynomials
\ell_{i}\in P_{k}
. That means
g
belongs to
\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}
, where
\mathcal{A}^{+}
is the augmentation ideal of
\mathcal{A}
.

We are interested in the
Peterson hit problem
of determining a minimal set of generators for the polynomial algebra
P_{k}
as a module over the Steenrod algebra,
\mathcal{A}
. That means, we need to determine a basis of the
\mathbb{F}_{2}
-vector space

QP_{k}:=P_{k}/\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}=\mathbb{F}_{2}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}P_{k}.

This problem was first studied by Peterson
[
8
]
, Wood
[
31
]
, Singer
[
17
]
, and Priddy
[
12
]
, who showed its relation to several classical problems in Algebraic Topology. Then, this problem was investigated by Carlisle and Wood
[
1
]
, Crabb and Hubbuck
[
2
]
, Janfada and Wood
[
3
]
, Ly and Tín
[
5
]
, Kameko
[
4
]
, Mothebe
[
6
]
, Nam
[
7
]
, Phúc and Sum
[
11
]
, Repka and Selick
[
13
]
, Silverman
[
14
,
15
]
, Silverman and Singer
[
16
]
, Singer
[
18
]
, Sum and Tín
[
26
]
, Tín
[
27
]
, Walker and Wood
[
28
]
, Wood
[
32
]
, the first named author
[
20
,
21
,
23
]
and others.

The hit problem was explicitly computed by
Peterson
[
8
]
for
k=1,2,
by Kameko
[
4
]
for
k=3
and by the first named author
[
21
]
for
k=4
. This problem is open for any
k>4
. Recently, the results on the hit problem and its applications to representations of general linear groups have been presented in the books of Walker and Wood
[
29
,
30
]
.

For any nonnegative integer
m
, Denote by
(P_{k})_{m}
and
(QP_{k})_{m}
the subspaces of degree
m
homogeneous polynomials in the spaces
P_{k}
and
QP_{k}
respectively. Set
\mu(m)=\min\{u\in\mathbb{Z}:\alpha(m+u)\leqslant u\}
, where
\alpha(a)
denotes the number of one in dyadic expansion of a positive integer
a
.

By combining the results in Kameko
[
4
]
and Wood
[
31
]
the hit problem is reduced to the case of degree
m
such that
\mu(m)<k
. For
\mu(m)=k-1
, the problem was studied by Crabb-Hubbuck
[
2
]
, Nam
[
7
]
, Repka-Selick
[
13
]
, Walker-Wood
[
28
]
and the first named author
[
20
,
21
]
. For
\mu(m)=k-2
, it was studied in
[
23
]
which provides a new tool for studying the Peterson hit problem.

In this paper, we compute the hit problem for the case
k=5
and the degrees
m=2^{d}
with
d\geqslant 8
in terms of the admissible monomials (see Section
2
). We prove the following.

Theorem 1.1
.

For any integer
d\geqslant 8
, there exist exactly
1984
admissible monomials of degree
2^{d}
in
P_{5}
. Consequently
\dim(QP_{5})_{2^{d}}=1984.

For the cases
d=1,\,2,\,3
, the computation is easy and we have
\dim(QP_{5})_{2}=10
,
\dim(QP_{5})_{4}=45
and
\dim(QP_{5})_{8}=174
. The cases
d=4,\,5
are respectively determined in our work
[
22
]
and Phúc
[
9
]
, we showed that
\dim(QP_{5})_{16}=443
and
\dim(QP_{5})_{32}=1004.
We also study the problem for
d=6,\,7
and we have
1679\leqslant\dim(QP_{5})_{64}\leqslant 1694
and
1984\leqslant\dim(QP_{5})_{128}\leqslant 1990
(see Section
5
).

The paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we recall some needed information on the admissible monomials in
P_{k}
, the criteria of Singer and Silverman on the hit monomials. The detailed proof of Theorem
1.1
is presented in Section
3
. In Section
4
we list the needed admissible monomials in
P_{5}
. Finally, in Section
5
we present the computations of
(QP_{5})_{2^{d}}
for
5\leqslant d\leqslant 7
.

2.
Preliminaries

In this section, we recall some needed information on the weight vector of a monomial and the admissible monomials from Kameko
[
4
]
, Singer
[
18
]
and the first named author
[
21
]
which will be used in the next section. We refer the reader to
[
21
]
and
[
23
]
for the details of notions and needed results.

Let
x=x_{1}^{a_{1}}x_{2}^{a_{2}}\ldots x_{k}^{a_{k}}\in P_{k}
. We denote
\nu_{j}(x)=a_{j},1\leqslant j\leqslant k
and
\nu(x)=\max\{\nu_{j}(x):1\leqslant j\leqslant k\}
. We define two sequences associated with
x
by

\displaystyle\omega(x)

\displaystyle=(\omega_{1}(x),\omega_{2}(x),\ldots,\omega_{i}(x),\ldots),\ \ \sigma(x)=(\nu_{1}(x),\nu_{2}(x),\ldots,\nu_{k}(x)),

where

\omega_{i}(x)=\sum_{1\leqslant j\leqslant k}\alpha_{i-1}(\nu_{j}(x)),\ i\geqslant 1.

The sequences
\omega(x)
and
\sigma(x)
are respectively called the weight vector and the exponent vector of
x
.

The sets of weight vectors and exponent vectors respectively are given the left lexicographical order.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
\omega
be a weight vector and
g,\,h
two polynomials of the same degree in
P_{k}
.

i)
g\equiv h
if and only if
g+h\in\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}
. If
g
is hit, then
g\equiv 0
.

ii)
g\equiv_{\omega}h
if and only if
g+h\in\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}+P_{k}^{-}(\omega)
.

It is easy to see that, the relations
\equiv
and
\equiv_{\omega}
are equivalence ones. Denote

QP_{k}(\omega)=P_{k}(\omega)/((\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}\cap P_{k}(\omega))+P_{k}^{-}(\omega)).

For a polynomial
g\in P_{k}
, denote by
[g]
the class in
QP_{k}
represented by
g
. If
\omega
is a weight vector and
g\in P_{k}(\omega)
, then we denote
[g]_{\omega}
the class in
QP_{k}(\omega)
represented by
g
. Denote by
|D|
the cardinal of a set
D
.

From
[
22
]
, for any degree
m
, we have

(QP_{k})_{m}\cong\bigoplus_{\deg\omega=m}QP_{k}(\omega).

(2.1)

Definition 2.2
.

Let
u,v
be monomials in
P_{k}
with
\deg u=\deg v
. We define
u<v
if and only if one of the following conditions holds:

i)
\omega(u)<\omega(v)
;

ii)
\omega(u)=\omega(v)
and
\sigma(u)<\sigma(v).

Definition 2.3
.

A monomial
u
in
P_{k}
is said to be inadmissible if there are monomials
v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{r}
such that
v_{j}<u
for
j=1,2,\ldots,r
and
u+\sum_{j=1}^{r}v_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}.

A monomial
u
is said to be admissible if it is not inadmissible.

Obviously, the set of all the admissible monomials of degree
m
in
P_{k}
is a minimal set of
\mathcal{A}
-generators for
P_{k}
in degree
m
.

Denote by
\mathcal{A}_{s}
the sub-Hopf algebra of
\mathcal{A}
generated by
Sq^{i}
with
0\leqslant i\leqslant 2^{s}
, and
\mathcal{A}_{s}^{+}=\mathcal{A}^{+}\cap\mathcal{A}_{s}
.

Definition 2.4
.

A monomial
u
in
P_{k}
is said to be strictly inadmissible if and only if there exist monomials
v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{r}
such that
v_{j}<u,
for
j=1,2,\ldots,r
and
u+\sum_{j=1}^{r}v_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{s-1}^{+}P_{k}
with
s=\max\{i:\omega_{i}(x)>0\}
.

Theorem 2.5

(See Kameko
[
4
]
, Sum
[
20
]
)
.

Let
u,v,w
be monomials in
P_{k}
such that
\omega_{i}(u)=0
for
i>r>0
,
\omega_{s}(w)\neq 0
and
\omega_{i}(w)=0
for
i>s>0
.

i)
If
w
is inadmissible, then so is
uw^{2^{r}}
.

ii)
If
w
is strictly inadmissible, then so is
wv^{2^{s}}
.

Proposition 2.6

(See
[
21
, Proposition 4.3]
)
.

Let
u
be an admissible monomial in
P_{k}
and let
t_{0}
be a positive integer. Then we have

i)
If
\omega_{t_{0}}(u)=0
, then
\omega_{t}(u)=0
for all
t>t_{0}
.

ii)
If
\omega_{t_{0}}(u)<k
, then
\omega_{t}(u)<k
for all
t>t_{0}
.

Now, we recall a result of Singer
[
18
]
on the hit monomials in
P_{k}
.

Definition 2.7
.

A monomial
z
in
P_{k}
is called a spike if
\nu_{j}(z)=2^{d_{j}}-1
for
d_{j}
a non-negative integer and
j=1,2,\ldots,k
. If
d_{1}>d_{2}>\ldots>d_{r-1}\geqslant d_{r}>0
and
d_{j}=0
for
j>r,
then
z
is called the minimal spike.

In
[
18
]
, Singer showed that if
\mu(m)\leqslant k
, then there exists uniquely a minimal spike of degree
m
in
P_{k}
.

Theorem 2.8

(See Singer
[
18
]
)
.

Suppose
u\in P_{k}
is a monomial of degree
m
, where
\mu(m)\leqslant k
. Let
z
be the minimal spike of degree
m
. If
\omega(u)<\omega(z)
, then
u\equiv 0
.

We set

\displaystyle P_{k}^{0}

\displaystyle=\langle\{x\in P_{k}\ :\ \prod_{j=1}^{k}\nu_{j}(x)=0\}\rangle,\ \ P_{k}^{+}=\langle\{x\in P_{k}\ :\ \prod_{j=1}^{k}\nu_{j}(x)>0\}\rangle.

It is easy to see that
P_{k}^{0}
and
P_{k}^{+}
are the
\mathcal{A}
-submodules of
P_{k}
and

QP_{k}=QP_{k}^{0}\oplus QP_{k}^{+}.

Here
QP_{k}^{0}=P_{k}^{0}/\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}^{0}
and
QP_{k}^{+}=P_{k}^{+}/\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}^{+}
.

We set

\mathcal{N}_{k}=\{(i;I):I=(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{r}),1\leqslant i<i_{1}<\ldots<i_{r}\leqslant k,\ 0\leqslant r<k\}.

For each
(i;I)\in\mathcal{N}_{k}
, we define the homomorphism of algebras
p_{(i;I)}:P_{k}\to P_{k-1}
by

p_{(i;I)}(x_{j})=\begin{cases}x_{j},&\text{ if }1\leqslant j<i,\\
\sum_{s\in I}x_{s-1},&\text{ if }j=i,\\
x_{j-1},&\text{ if }i<j\leqslant k.\end{cases}

(2.2)

Then
p_{(i;I)}
is a homomorphism of
\mathcal{A}
-algebras.

Lemma 2.9

(Phúc-Sum
[
10
]
)
.

For any monomial
u
in
P_{k}
, we have

p_{(i;I)}(u)\in P_{k-1}(\omega(u)).

It is easy to see that if
\omega
is a weight vector of degree
m
and
u\in P_{k}(\omega)
, then
p_{(i;I)}(u)\in P_{k-1}(\omega)
. Moreover,
p_{(i;I)}
passes to a homomorphism

\displaystyle p_{(i;I)}^{(\omega)}:QP_{k}(\omega)\longrightarrow QP_{k-1}(\omega),\ \ p_{(i;I)}:(QP_{k})_{m}\longrightarrow(QP_{k-1})_{m}.

We set

\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega)=\bigcap_{(i;I)\in\mathcal{N}_{k}}\mbox{Ker}(p_{(i;I)}^{(\omega)})\mbox{ and }\widetilde{QP}_{k}(\omega)=QP_{k}(\omega)/\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega).

We see that
\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega)
is a subspace of the spike-free module
SF^{\omega}_{k}
(see Walker and Wood
[
30
, Chap. 30]
). Then we have

QP_{k}(\omega)\cong\widetilde{QP}_{k}(\omega)\bigoplus\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega).

For
J=(j_{1},j_{2},\ldots,j_{s}):1\leqslant j_{1}<\ldots<j_{s}\leqslant k
, we define a monomorphism of
\mathcal{A}
-algebras
\theta_{J}:P_{s}\to P_{k}
by substituting

\theta_{J}(x_{t})=x_{j_{t}}\ \mbox{ for }\ 1\leqslant t\leqslant s.

(2.3)

Obviously, for any weight vector
\omega
of degree
m
,

Q\theta_{J}(P_{s}^{+})(\omega)\cong QP_{s}^{+}(\omega)\mbox{ and }(Q\theta_{J}(P_{s}^{+}))_{m}\cong(QP_{s}^{+})_{m}

for
1\leqslant s\leqslant k
, where
Q\theta_{J}(P_{s}^{+})=\theta_{J}(P_{s}^{+})/\mathcal{A}^{+}\theta_{J}(P_{s}^{+})
. Hence, by a simple computation using the result in Wood
[
31
]
and (
2.1
), we get the following.

Proposition 2.10

(See Walker and Wood
[
29
]
)
.

Let
\omega
be a weight vector of degree
m
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\dim QP_{k}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu(m)\leqslant s\leqslant k}{k\choose s}\dim QP_{s}^{+}(\omega),

\displaystyle\dim(QP_{k})_{m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu(m)\leqslant s\leqslant k}{k\choose s}\dim(QP_{s}^{+})_{m}.

Set
J_{t}=(1,\ldots,\hat{t},\ldots,k)
for
1\leqslant t\leqslant k
.

Proposition 2.11

(See Mothebe and Uys
[
6
]
)
.

Let
t,d
be positive integers such that
1\leqslant t\leqslant k
. If
u
is an admissible monomial in
P_{k-1}
then so is
x_{t}^{2^{d}-1}\theta_{J_{t}}(u)
in
P_{k}
.

Notation 2.12
.

Denote by
B_{k}(m)
the set of all admissible monomials of degree
m
in
P_{k}
. We set

B_{k}^{0}(m)=B_{k}(m)\cap P_{k}^{0},\ B_{k}^{+}(m)=B_{k}(m)\cap P_{k}^{+}.

For any weight vector
\omega
of degree
m
, we denote

B_{k}(\omega)=B_{k}(m)\cap P_{k}(\omega),\ B_{k}^{+}(\omega)=B_{k}^{+}(m)\cap P_{k}(\omega).

Let
D
be a subset in
P_{k}
. We denote
[D]=\{[f]:f\in D\}
. For
D\subset P_{k}(\omega)
, we set
[D]_{\omega}=\{[f]_{\omega}:f\in D\}
.
Then,
[B_{k}(\omega)]_{\omega}
and
[B_{k}^{+}(\omega)]_{\omega}
, are the basses of the
\mathbb{F}_{2}
-vector spaces
QP_{k}(\omega)
and
QP_{k}^{+}(\omega):=QP_{k}(\omega)\cap QP_{k}^{+}
respectively.

3.
The indecomposables of
P_{5}
in degree
2^{d}

3.1.
The weight vector of admissible monomials of degree
2^{d}

First of all, we determine the weight vectors of the admissible monomials of degree
2^{d}
for
d\geqslant 5
.

Lemma 3.1.1
.

If
x
be an admissible monomial of degree
2^{d}
in
P_{5}
for
d\geqslant 5
, then either
\omega(x)=(2)|(1)|^{d-1}
or
\omega(x)=(4)|(2)|^{d-2}
or
\omega(x)=(4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}|(1)
.

Proof.

The lemma is proved by Phúc
[
9
]
for
d=5
. Observe that
z=x_{1}^{2^{d}-1}x_{2}
is the minimal spike of degree
2^{d}
in
P_{5}
and
\omega(z)=(2)|(1)|^{d-1}
. Since
2^{d}
is even, using Theorem
2.8
, we obtain
\omega_{1}(x)=2
or
\omega_{1}(x)=4
.

If
\omega_{1}(x)=2
, then
x=x_{i}x_{j}y^{2}
with
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant 5
and
y
an admissible monomial of degree
2^{d-1}-1
. By using Proposition
2.6
and
[
24
, Lemma 4.1]
we get either
\omega(y)=(1)|^{d-1}
or
\omega(y)=(3)|(2)|^{d-3}
or
\omega(y)=(3,4,3,1)
for
d=6
. If
\omega(y)=(3,4,3,1)
, then
x=wu^{8}
with
w
a monomial of weight vector
(2,3,4)
and
u
a monomial of weight vector
(3,1)
. Since
\mu(24)=4
, by Theorem
2.8

w
is hit, hence
x
is inadmissible. If
\omega(y)=(3)|(2)|^{d-3}
, then
x=w_{1}u_{1}^{16}
, with
w_{1}
a monomial of weight vector
(2,3,2,2)
and
u_{1}
a monomial of weight vector
(2)|^{d-5}
. From Phúc
[
9
]
we see that
w_{1}
is strictly inadmissible. By Theorem
2.5
,
x
is inadmissible. Thus,
\omega(y)=(1)|^{d-1}
and
\omega(x)=(2)|(1)|^{d-1}
.

If
\omega_{1}(x)=4
. Then
x=X_{i}y^{2}
with
y
an admissible monomial of degree
2^{d-1}-2
in
P_{5}
and
1\leqslant i\leqslant 5
. From
[
24
, Lemma 3.1]
and
[
25
]
we see that either
\omega(y)=(2)|^{d-2}
or
\omega(y)=(4)|(2)|^{d-4}|(1)
or
y=x_{1}^{3}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{6}x_{4}^{6}x_{5}^{10}
for
d=6
. By a direct computation we see that if
y=x_{1}^{3}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{6}x_{4}^{6}x_{5}^{10}
, then
x=X_{i}y^{2}
is strictly inadmissible. So, we obtain either
\omega(x)=(4)|(2)|^{d-2}
or
\omega(y)=(4)|^{2}||(3)|^{d-4}|(1)
. The lemma is proved.
∎

By Lemma
3.1.1
, we obtain

Corollary 3.1.2
.

For any integer
d\geqslant 5
, we have

\displaystyle(QP_{5})_{2^{d}}=(QP_{5}^{0})_{2^{d}}

\displaystyle\oplus QP_{5}^{+}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})

\displaystyle\oplus QP_{5}^{+}((4)|(2)|^{d-2})\oplus QP_{5}^{+}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}|(1)).

3.2.
Computation of
QP_{5}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})

From Kameko
[
4
]
and our work
[
21
]
, for any
d\geqslant 5
, we have

B_{2}^{+}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})=\left\{x_{1}x_{2}^{2^{d}-1},\,x_{1}^{2^{d}-1}x_{2},\,x_{1}^{3}x_{2}^{2^{d}-3}\right\}
,

B_{3}^{+}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})=\left\{x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}^{2^{d}-2},\,x_{1}x_{2}^{2^{d}-2}x_{3},\,x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2^{d}-3},\,x_{1}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{2^{d}-4},\,x_{1}^{3}x_{2}x_{3}^{2^{d}-4}\right\}
,

B_{4}^{+}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})=\left\{u_{t}=u_{d,t}:1\leqslant t\leqslant 7\right\}
, where

u_{1}=x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{2^{d}-4}

u_{2}=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}x_{4}^{2^{d}-4}

u_{3}=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2^{d}-4}x_{4}

u_{4}=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{4}x_{4}^{2^{d}-7}

u_{5}=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{5}x_{4}^{2^{d}-8}

u_{6}=x_{1}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{4}x_{4}^{2^{d}-8}

u_{7}=x_{1}^{3}x_{2}x_{3}^{4}x_{4}^{2^{d}-8}
.

Hence, we get

\displaystyle\dim QP_{5}^{0}((2)|^{d})=3{5\choose 2}+5{5\choose 3}+7{5\choose 4}=115.

So, we need to determine the space
QP_{5}^{+}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})
.
In this subsection we prove the following.

Theorem 3.2.1
.

For any
d\geqslant 5
,
B_{5}^{+}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})=\{a_{d,t}:\,1\leqslant t\leqslant 9\}
, where the monomials
a_{t}=a_{d,t}
are determined as follows:

a_{1}=x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{4}x_{5}^{2^{d}-8}

a_{2}=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}x_{4}^{4}x_{5}^{2^{d}-8}

a_{3}=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{4}x_{4}x_{5}^{2^{d}-8}

a_{4}=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{4}x_{4}^{2^{d}-8}x_{5}

a_{5}=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{4}x_{4}^{8}x_{5}^{2^{d}-15}

a_{6}=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{4}x_{4}^{9}x_{5}^{2^{d}-16}

a_{7}=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{5}x_{4}^{8}x_{5}^{2^{d}-16}

a_{8}=x_{1}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{4}x_{4}^{8}x_{5}^{2^{d}-16}

a_{9}=x_{1}^{3}x_{2}x_{3}^{4}x_{4}^{8}x_{5}^{2^{d}-16}
.

Consequently,

\dim QP_{5}^{+}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})=9,\ \dim QP_{5}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})=124.

We need a lemma for the proof of the theorem.

Lemma 3.2.2

(See
[
22
]
)
.

Let
(i,j,t,u,v)
be an arbitrary permutation of
(1,2,3,4,5)
. The following monomials are strictly inadmissible:

​
i)

x_{i}^{2}x_{j}x_{t},\,x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{12}x_{t},\,x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{5}x_{t}^{8},\,x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{4}x_{t}^{9},\ i<j<t.

ii)

x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{4}x_{i}x_{u}^{8},\,x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{4}x_{i}^{8}x_{u},\ i<j<t<u
.

Proof of Theorem
3.2.1
.

Let
x
be an admissible monomial of the weight vector
(2)|(1)|^{d-1}
in
P_{5}^{+}
with
d\geqslant 5
. Then
x=x_{i}x_{j}y^{2}
with
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant 5
and
y
an admissible monomial of the weight vector
(1)|^{d-1}
. By a direct computation we see that if
z
is an admissible monomial of weight vector
(1)|^{d-1}
and
x_{i}x_{j}z^{2}\neq a_{d,t}
for all
t,\,1\leqslant t\leqslant 9
, then there is a monomial
w
as given in Lemma
3.2.2
such that
x=wz_{1}^{2^{r}}
with
r
an integer,
2\leqslant r\leqslant 4
, and
z_{1}
a suitable monomial of the weight vector
(1)|^{d-r}
. By Theorem
2.5
,
x_{i}x_{j}z^{2}
is inadmissible. Since
x=x_{i}x_{j}y^{2}
is admissible, we have
x=a_{d,t}
for some
t,\,1\leqslant t\leqslant 9
. Hence,

B_{5}^{+}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})\subset\{a_{d,t}:1\leqslant t\leqslant 9\}.

We now prove the set
\{[a_{d,t}]:1\leqslant t\leqslant 9\}
is linearly independent in the vector space
QP_{5}((2)|(1)|^{d-1})\subset QP_{5}
. Suppose there is a linear relation

\mathcal{S}:=\sum_{1\leqslant t\leqslant 9}\gamma_{t}a_{d,t}\equiv 0,

(3.1)

where
\gamma_{t}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}
. We denote
\gamma_{\mathbb{J}}=\sum_{t\in\mathbb{J}}\gamma_{t}
for any
\mathbb{J}\subset\{t\in\mathbb{N}:1\leqslant t\leqslant 9\}
.

Consider the homomorphism
p_{(i;I)}:P_{5}\to P_{4}
defined by (
2.2
) for
k=5
. By applying
p_{(1;2)}
to (
3.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle p_{(1;2)}(\mathcal{S})\equiv\gamma_{\{3,4,5,6,7\}}

\displaystyle u_{d,1}+\gamma_{3}u_{d,2}+\gamma_{4}u_{d,3}+\gamma_{5}u_{d,4}

\displaystyle+\gamma_{6}u_{d,5}+\gamma_{7}u_{d,6}+\gamma_{\{2,3,4,5,6,7\}}u_{d,7}\equiv 0.

(3.2)

From (
3.2
), we get

\gamma_{t}=0\mbox{ for }t\in\{2,\,3,\,4,\,5,\,6,\,7\}.

(3.3)

Applying
p_{(1;3)}
to (
3.1
) and using (
3.3
) we obtain

\displaystyle p_{(1;3)}(\mathcal{S})

\displaystyle\equiv\gamma_{8}u_{d,1}+\gamma_{\{1,8,9\}}u_{d,6}+\gamma_{8}u_{d,7}\equiv 0.

(3.4)

The relation (
3.4
) implies

\gamma_{8}=0\mbox{ and }\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{9}.

(3.5)

By a simple computation using (
3.3
) and (
3.5
) we obtain

\displaystyle p_{(2;3)}(\mathcal{S})

\displaystyle\equiv\gamma_{1}u_{d,1}+\gamma_{1}u_{d,7}\equiv 0.

(3.6)

From (
3.6
) it implies
\gamma_{1}=0
. Hence, we get
\gamma_{t}=0
for all
t,\ 1\leqslant t\leqslant 9
. The proof is completed.
∎

The above equalities show that the monomials
x,\,y,\,z,\,t
are strictly inadmissible.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.3.1
.

Let
A(d)
and
B(d)
be as in Subsection
4.1
with
d\geqslant 6
and let
x\in P_{5}^{+}(\omega)
be an admissible monomial with
\omega:=(4)|(2)|^{d-2}
, then
x=X_{i}y^{2}
with
1\leqslant i\leqslant 5
and
y
a monomial of weight vector
(2)|^{d-2}
. Since
x
is admissible, by Theorem
2.5
,
y
is admissible. By a direct computation we see that if
\bar{y}
is an admissible monomial of weight vector
(2)|^{d-2}
and
X_{i}\bar{y}^{2}\notin A(d)\cup B(d)
, then there is a monomial
w
as given in one of Lemmas
3.3.2
or
3.3.3
such that
X_{i}\bar{y}^{2}=wz^{2^{r}}
with
r
an integer,
2\leqslant r\leqslant 6
, and
z
a monomial of weight vector
(2)|^{d-r}
. By Theorem
2.5
,
X_{i}\bar{y}^{2}
is inadmissible. Since
x=X_{i}y^{2}
with
y
an admissible monomial of weight vector
(2)|^{d-2}
, we get
x\in A(d)\cup B(d)
and
B_{5}^{+}(\omega)\subset A(d)\cup B(d)
.

Now we prove that the set
[A(d)\cup B(d)]_{\omega}
is linearly independent in
QP_{5}(\omega)
.

Consider the subspaces
\langle[A(d)]_{\omega}\rangle\subset QP_{5}(\omega)
and
\langle[B(d)]_{\omega}\rangle\subset QP_{5}(\omega)
. It is easy to see that for any
x\in A(d)
, we have
x=\theta_{J_{i}}(\bar{x})
with
\bar{x}
an admissible monomial of weight vector
(4)|(2)|^{d-2}
in
P_{4}
. By Proposition
2.11
,
x
is admissible. This implies
\dim\langle[A(d)]_{\omega}\rangle=85
. Since
\nu(x)=2^{d-1}-1
for all
x\in A(d)
and
\nu(x)<2^{d-1}-1
for all
x\in B(d)
, we obtain
\langle[A(d)]_{\omega}\rangle\cap\langle[B(d)]_{\omega}\rangle=\{0\}
. Hence, we need only to prove the set
[B(d)]_{\omega}=\{[b_{d,t}]_{\omega}:1\leqslant t\leqslant 205\}
is linearly independent in
QP_{5}(\omega)
, where the monomials
b_{d,t}:1\leqslant t\leqslant 205
, are determined as in Subsection
4.1
.

Suppose there is a linear relation

\mathcal{S}:=\sum_{1\leqslant t\leqslant 205}\gamma_{t}b_{d,t}\equiv_{\omega}0,

(3.7)

where
\gamma_{t}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}
. We denote
\gamma_{\mathbb{J}}=\sum_{t\in\mathbb{J}}\gamma_{t}
for any
\mathbb{J}\subset\{t\in\mathbb{N}:1\leqslant t\leqslant 75\}
.

3.4.
Computation of
QP_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}|(1))

Let
d\geqslant 8
. From Proposition
3.4.2
below and the monomials as given in Subsection
4.2
, we see that for any
x\in B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4})
there exists uniquely a sequence
J_{x}=(j_{1}^{x},j_{2}^{x},j_{3}^{x})
such that
1\leqslant j_{1}^{x}<j_{2}^{x}<j_{3}^{x}\leqslant 5
and
\nu_{j_{s}^{x}}(x)>32
for
s=1,\,2,\,3
. Consider the homomorphism
\theta_{J}:P_{r}\to P_{k}
defined by (
2.3
) for
r=3,\,k=5
. The main result of this subsection is the following.

Theorem 3.4.1
.

For any
d\geqslant 8
, we have

B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-2}|(1))=\left\{x\theta_{J_{x}}\big(x_{u}^{2^{d-2}}\big):x\in B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}),\,u=1,\,2,\,3\right\}.

Consequently,

\dim QP_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-2}|(1))=1395.

Proof.

Let
y
be an admissible monomial of weight vector
(4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}|(1)
, then
y=X_{i}\bar{x}^{2}x_{t}^{2^{d}-2}
with
1\leqslant i,\,t\leqslant 5
and
\bar{x}
an admissible monomial of weight vector
(4)|(3)|^{d-4}
. From our work
[
24
, Proposition 3.3.1]
we see that there exists uniquely a sequence
J_{\bar{x}}=(j_{1}^{\bar{x}},j_{2}^{\bar{x}},j_{3}^{\bar{x}})
such that
1\leqslant j_{1}^{\bar{x}}<j_{2}^{\bar{x}}<j_{3}^{\bar{x}}\leqslant 5
and
\nu_{j_{s}^{\bar{x}}}(\bar{x})>16
for
s=1,\,2,\,3
and
x_{t}=\theta_{J_{\bar{x}}}(x_{u})
for suitable
1\leqslant u\leqslant 3
. Then, by Theorem
2.5
,
x=X_{i}\bar{x}^{2}
is an admissible monomial of weit vector
(4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}
,
J_{x}=J_{\bar{x}}
and
\nu_{j_{s}^{x}}(x)>32
for
s=1,\,2,\,3
. Thus, we have proved that

B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-2}|(1))\subset\left\{x\theta_{J_{x}}\left(x_{u}^{2^{d-2}}\right):x\in B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}),\,u=1,\,2,\,3\right\}.

We now prove that the set
\left\{\big[x\theta_{J_{x}}\big(x_{u}^{2^{d-2}}\big)\big]_{\omega}:x\in B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}),\,u=1,\,2,\,3\right\}
is linearly independent in
QP_{5}(\omega)
with
\omega:=(4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}|(1)
. Suppose there is a linear relation

\mathcal{S}:=\sum_{x\in B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4})\atop u=1,2,3}\gamma_{x,u}x\theta_{J_{x}}\big(x_{u}^{2^{d-2}}\big)\equiv_{\omega}0,

(3.13)

with
\gamma_{x,u}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}
. Consider the set
B_{4}(\omega)
as given in
[
21
]
and the homomorphism
p_{(i;I)}:P_{5}\to P_{4}
as defined by (
2.2
) for
k=5
. We compute
p_{(i;I)}(\mathcal{S})
in terms of the elements in
B_{4}(\omega)
(mod(
P_{4}^{-}(\omega)+\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{4}
). From the relation
p_{(i;I)}(\mathcal{S})\equiv_{\omega}0
with all
(i,I)\in\mathcal{N}_{5}
we obtain
\gamma_{x,u}=0
for all
x\in B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4})
and
u=1,2,3
. The theorem is proved. ∎

In the remaining part of this subsection, we explicitly determine the space
QP_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4})
for any
d\geqslant 8
.

From our work
[
21
]
, we have
|B_{4}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4})|=|B_{4}^{+}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4})|=15
, hence we get
|B_{5}^{0}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4})|=15{5\choose 4}=75
. In this subsection we prove the following.

Proposition 3.4.2
.

For any
d\geqslant 8
,
B_{5}^{+}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4})
is the set of
390
admissible monomials which are determined as in Subsection
4.2
. Consequently,

\dim QP_{5}^{+}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4})=390,\ \dim QP_{5}((4)|(3)|^{d-4})=465.

The proposition is proved by using Theorems
2.5
,
2.8
and some technical lemmas.

Lemma 3.4.3
.

Let
(i,j,t,u,v)
be an arbitrary permutation of
(1,2,3,4,5)
. The following monomials are strictly inadmissible:

x_{i}^{2}xjx_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{3}x_{v}^{3},\,i<j;\ x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{4}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{7}x_{v}^{7},\,i<j<t.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle x_{i}^{2}xjx_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{3}x_{v}^{3}=x_{i}xj^{2}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{3}x_{v}^{3}+Sq^{1}(x_{i}xjx_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{3}x_{v}^{3})\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2})\big).

Since
i<j
, the monomial
x_{i}^{2}xjx_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{3}x_{v}^{3}
is strictly inadmissible.

\displaystyle x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{4}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{7}x_{v}^{7}

\displaystyle=x_{i}^{2}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{5}x_{u}^{7}x_{v}^{7}+x_{i}^{2}x_{j}^{5}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{7}x_{v}^{7}+x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{4}x_{u}^{7}x_{v}^{7}

\displaystyle\quad+Sq^{1}(x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{7}x_{v}^{7})+Sq^{2}(x_{i}^{2}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{7}x_{v}^{7})\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2}|(3)\big).

Since
i<j<t
, the monomial
x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{4}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{7}x_{v}^{7}
is strictly inadmissible. The lemma is proved.
∎

Lemma 3.4.4
.

Let
(i,j,t,u,v)
be an arbitrary permutation of
(1,2,3,4,5)
. The following monomials are strictly inadmissible:

​
i)

x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{9}x_{t}^{2}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15},\,x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{8}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15},\,x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{8}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15},\,x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{8}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15},\ i<j<t.

ii)

x_{r}^{15}\theta_{J_{r}}(w)
,

, with
w
one of the monomials:

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{8}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{11},\,x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{3}x_{4}^{14},\,x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{6}x_{4}^{11},\,x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{10},\,x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{14}x_{4}^{3},\,x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{8}.

iii)

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{11},\,x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{14}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{11}
.

Proof.

Each monomial in the lemma is of weight vector
(4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}
. We prove the lemma for some monomials. The others can be proved by a similar computation. By using the Cartan formula, we have

\displaystyle x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{8}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}=x_{i}^{2}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{9}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{2}x_{j}^{9}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{4}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{1}\big(x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)+Sq^{2}\big(x_{i}^{2}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{4}\big(x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{4}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{2}\big),

\displaystyle x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{8}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}=x_{i}^{4}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{5}x_{j}^{2}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{2}x_{t}^{9}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{1}\big(x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)+Sq^{2}\big(x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{2}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{4}\big(x_{i}^{4}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{5}x_{j}^{2}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{2}\big),

\displaystyle x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{8}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}=x_{i}^{5}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{10}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{5}x_{j}^{10}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{8}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{1}\big(x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{5}x_{t}^{5}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)+Sq^{2}\big(x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{6}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{6}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{4}\big(x_{i}^{5}x_{j}^{6}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{5}x_{j}^{3}x_{t}^{6}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{2}\big),

\displaystyle x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{9}x_{t}^{2}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}=x_{i}^{4}x_{j}^{11}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{5}x_{j}^{11}x_{t}^{2}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{8}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{1}\big(x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{4}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)+Sq^{2}\big(x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{2}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{4}\big(x_{i}^{4}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}+x_{i}^{5}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{2}x_{u}^{15}x_{v}^{15}\big)\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{2}\big).

Since
i<j<t
, the monomials in Part i) are strictly inadmissible.

We prove Part ii) for
x=x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{3}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{15}
and

. We have

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{3}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{6}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{15}

\displaystyle\quad+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{10}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{10}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{8}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{10}x_{4}^{9}x_{5}^{15}

\displaystyle\quad+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{8}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{8}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}+Sq^{1}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{5}x_{4}^{13}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+Sq^{2}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{6}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{23}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{3}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{6}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{10}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+Sq^{4}\big(x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{3}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{6}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{10}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}

\displaystyle\quad+x_{1}^{11}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{6}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}\big)+Sq^{8}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{6}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}\big)\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{2}\big),

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{10}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{9}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{10}x_{3}^{9}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}

\displaystyle\quad+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{10}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{10}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{8}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{4}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{15}

\displaystyle\quad+Sq^{1}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}\big)+Sq^{2}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{10}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{15}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{23}\big)

The above equalities show that the monomials

are strictly inadmissible.

Based on the Cartan formula we get

\displaystyle x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{11}=x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{14}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{11}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{14}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{11}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{9}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{14}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{9}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{11}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{14}

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{1}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{13}x_{5}^{13}\big)+Sq^{2}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{14}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{11}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{4}\big(x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{14}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{11}\big)\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{2}\big).

This equality shows that the monomial
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{11}
is strictly inadmissible.
∎

Lemma 3.4.5
.

The following monomials are strictly inadmissible:

​
i)

x_{r}^{31}\theta_{J_{r}}(w)
,
1\leqslant r\leqslant 5
, with
w
one of the monomials:

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{24}

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{27}

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{26}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{27}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{26}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{19}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{18}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{19}x_{4}^{16}
.

ii)

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}
,
x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}
,
x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{19}
,
x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{21}x_{5}^{26}
.

Proof.

It is easy to see that each monomial in this lemma is of weight vector
(4)|^{2}(3)|^{3}
. We prove the lemma for some monomials. The others can be proved by a similar computation.

We prove Part i) for

and

. A direct computation using the Cartan formula gives

\displaystyle x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{24}x_{5}^{31}=x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{30}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{6}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{10}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{19}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{22}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{26}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{25}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{30}x_{4}^{25}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{26}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{6}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{24}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{26}x_{4}^{25}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{1}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{29}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{21}x_{4}^{29}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{2}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{39}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{38}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{22}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{39}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{23}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{30}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{6}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{19}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{22}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{26}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}\big)+Sq^{4}\big(x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{23}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{30}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{6}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{19}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{22}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{26}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{11}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{11}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{22}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{8}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{22}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{31}\big)\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{3}\big)

\displaystyle x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{26}x_{5}^{31}=x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{19}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{19}x_{4}^{26}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{26}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{8}x_{3}^{23}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{23}x_{4}^{26}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+Sq^{1}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}\big)+Sq^{2}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{26}x_{5}^{31}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{4}\big(x_{1}^{4}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{26}x_{5}^{31}+Sq^{8}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{8}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{31}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{26}x_{5}^{31}\big)\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{3}\big).

Hence, the monomials
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{27}x_{4}^{24}x_{5}^{31},\,x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{26}x_{5}^{31}
are strictly inadmissible.

We prove Part ii) for
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}
. We have

\displaystyle x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}=x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{19}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{30}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{19}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{30}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{23}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{30}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{23}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{30}+Sq^{1}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{29}x_{5}^{29}\big)+Sq^{2}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{30}

\displaystyle\qquad+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}\big)+Sq^{4}\big(x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{30}+x_{1}^{5}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+Sq^{8}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{27}x_{5}^{30}+x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}\big)\ \mbox{mod}\big(P_{5}^{-}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{3}\big).

This equality implies that the monomial
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{27}
is strictly inadmissible.
∎

Lemma 3.4.6
.

The following monomials are strictly inadmissible:

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{55}x_{4}^{59}x_{5}^{56}

Proof of Proposition
3.4.2
.

Let
\bar{A}(d)
and
\bar{B}(d)
be as in Subsection
4.2
with
d\geqslant 8
and let
x\in P_{5}^{+}(\bar{\omega})
be an admissible monomial with
\bar{\omega}:=(4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}
. Then
x=X_{r}y^{2}
with
1\leqslant r\leqslant 5
and
y
an admissible monomial of weight vector
(4)|(3)|^{d-4}
. By a direct computation we see that if
x\notin\bar{A}(d)\cup\bar{B}(d)
, then there is a monomial
w
as given in one of Lemmas
3.4.3
,
3.4.4
,
3.4.5
and
3.4.6
such that

with

nonnegative integers,
2\leqslant u\leqslant 6
, and
z_{1}
a monomial of weight vector
(3)|^{d-u}
. By Theorem
2.5
,
x
is inadmissible. This contradicts the fact that
x
is admissible. Hence,
B_{5}^{+}(\bar{\omega})\subset\bar{A}(d)\cup\bar{B}(d)
.

Now we prove that the set
[\bar{A}(d)\cup\bar{B}(d)]_{\bar{\omega}}
is linearly independent in
QP_{5}(\bar{\omega})
.

Consider the subspaces

and

. It is easy to see that for any

, we have

with

an admissible monomial of weight vector

in

. By Proposition
2.11
,

is admissible. This implies

. Since

for all

and
\nu(x)<2^{d-2}-1
for all

. Hence, we need only to prove the set

QP_{5}(\bar{\omega})
, where the monomials

, are determined as in Subsection
4.2
.

(3.19)

p_{(1;(2,3,4,5))}
to (
3.7
) and using (
3.15
)-(
3.19
), we get

\displaystyle p_{(1;(2,3,4,5))}\equiv_{\bar{\omega}}\gamma_{\{21,34,35,36,74,76,78,80\}}w_{1}+\gamma_{\{29,60,95,97\}}w_{2}+\gamma_{101}w_{3}+\gamma_{104}w_{4}

\displaystyle\ +\gamma_{\{9,16,21,28,34,37,38,39,40,41,42,43,45,46,47,50,51,52,60,63,65,74,76,78,80,88,91,92,95,97,100\}}w_{5}

\displaystyle\ +\gamma_{\{10,35,37,39,40,74,76,78,83,84,87,101\}}w_{6}+\gamma_{\{11,21,36,38,41,80,85,87,88,104\}}w_{7}

\displaystyle\ +\gamma_{\{29,63,69,95,99,100,101,103\}}w_{8}+\gamma_{\{65,97,99,104\}}w_{9}+\gamma_{103}w_{10}+\gamma_{\{55,88,91,103\}}w_{13}

\displaystyle\ +\gamma_{\{2,12,15,16,17,21,29,42,43,45,46,50,51,54,55,69,74,76,78,83,84,87,88,91,92,95,99,101,103\}}w_{11}

\displaystyle\ +\gamma_{\{19,21,47,52,54,80,85,87,91,92,97,99,100,104\}}w_{12}+\gamma_{100}w_{14}+\gamma_{92}w_{15}\equiv_{\bar{\omega}}0.

By computing from the above equalities we obtain
\gamma_{t}=0
for all
t,\ 1\leqslant t\leqslant 115
. The proposition is proved.
∎

.

4.3.
The admissible monomials of weight vector
(4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}|(1)
in
P_{5}

We observe that for any
x\in|B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4})
there exists unique
J_{x}=(j_{1}^{x},j_{2}^{x},j_{3}^{x})
such that
1\leqslant j_{1}^{x}<j_{2}^{x}<j_{3}^{x}\leqslant 5
and
\nu_{j_{t}^{x}}(x)>32
for
t=1,\,2,\,3
. Then we have

B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}|(1))=\left\{x\theta_{J_{x}}\big(x_{s}^{2^{d-2}}\big):x\in B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}),\,s=1,\,2,\,3\right\}.

Thus, we obtain
|B_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{d-4}|(1))|=1395
and

|B_{5}(2^{d})|=124+465+1395=1984,

for any
d\geqslant 8
.

5.
Appendix 2

In this section we present some computations of
(QP_{5})_{2^{d}}
for
5\leqslant d\leqslant 7
.

From Lemma
2.9
, it implies that if
\omega
is a weight vector of degree
n
and
x\in P_{k}(\omega)
, then
p_{(i;I)}(x)\in P_{k-1}(\omega)
. Moreover,
p_{(i;I)}
passes to a homomorphism

\displaystyle p_{(i;I)}^{(\omega)}:QP_{k}(\omega)\longrightarrow QP_{k-1}(\omega).

We set

\displaystyle\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{(i;I)\in\mathcal{N}_{k}}\mbox{Ker}(p_{(i;I)}^{(\omega)})\subset\widetilde{QP}_{k}^{+}(\omega)

\displaystyle\widetilde{QP}_{k}(\omega)

\displaystyle=QP_{k}(\omega)/\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega),\ \widetilde{QP}_{k}^{+}(\omega)=QP_{k}^{+}(\omega)/\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega).

We see that
\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega)
is a subspace of the spike-free module
SF^{\omega}_{k}
(see Walker and Wood
[
30
, Chap. 30]
). Then we have

QP_{k}(\omega)\cong\widetilde{QP}_{k}(\omega)\bigoplus\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega).

5.1.
A note on the structure of the space
(QP_{5})_{32}

By Lemma
3.1.1
, we have

This result shows that the proof in Phúc
[
9
, Pages 22-23]
for
QP_{5}((4)|(2)|^{3})
is refused. If the result in
[
9
]
for
QP_{5}((4)|(2)|^{3})
is true then
\dim\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|(2)|^{3})=20
. However, we unable to prove this by hand computation. We think that this can be checked by using a computer calculation.

It is well-known that the monomials
x_{1}^{3}x_{2}^{5}x_{3}^{8}x_{4}
,
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{8}x_{4}^{9}
,
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{9}x_{4}^{8}
are admissible. So, by using Proposition
2.11
, the leading monomials of the polynomials
\theta_{9}
,
\theta_{10}
,
\theta_{11}
and
\theta_{t},\,16\leqslant t\leqslant 20
are admissible. Hence, from the above equalities and a simple calculation, we obtain the following.

Proposition 5.1.1
.

We have

i)

\dim\widetilde{QP}_{k}((4)|(2)|^{3})=465
.

ii)

\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|(2)|^{3})
is an
GL_{5}
-submodule of
GL_{5}
-module
{QP}_{k}((4)|(2)|^{3})
and

8\leqslant\dim\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|(2)|^{3})\leqslant 20.

5.2.
Computation of
(QP_{5})_{64}

By Lemma
3.1.1
, we have

(QP_{5})_{64}\cong QP_{5}((2)|(1)|^{5})\bigoplus QP_{5}((4)|(2)|^{4})\bigoplus QP_{5}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}|(1)).

By Theorems
3.2.1
and
3.3.1
, we have

\dim QP_{5}((2)|(1)|^{4})=124,\ \dim QP_{5}((4)|(2)|^{4})=465.

Hence, we need to compute
QP_{5}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}|(1))
.

5.2.1.
Computation of
QP_{5}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}|(1))

By using a result in
[
21
]
, we have
B_{4}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}|(1))=B_{4}^{+}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}|(1))
and
|B_{4}^{+}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}|(1))|=35
. This implies
\dim QP_{5}^{0}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}|(1))=5\times 35=175.
Hence, we need only to compute
QP_{5}^{+}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}|(1))
.
We will prove the following

Proposition 5.2.1
.

B_{5}^{+}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{2}|(1))
is the set contained
915
admissible monomials
g_{j},\,1\leqslant j\leqslant 915
, which are determined as in Subsubsection
5.2.2
. Consequently,

\dim QP_{5}^{+}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{2}|(1))\geqslant 915,\ \dim QP_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{2}|(1))\geqslant 1090.

Lemma 5.2.2
.

The following monomials are strictly inadmissible:

Proof.

We prove the lemma for
w_{1}=x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{9}x_{3}^{3}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{31}

Proof of Proposition
5.2.1
.

Let
x\in P_{5}^{+}(\omega)
be an admissible monomial with
\omega:=(4)|^{2}|(3)|^{2}|(1)
. Then
x=X_{r}y^{2}
with
1\leqslant r\leqslant 5
and
y
an admissible monomial of weight vector
(4)|(3)|^{2}|(1)
. By a direct computation we see that if
x\neq g_{j},\,1\leqslant j\leqslant 930
, then either
x
is one of monomials as given in Lemma
5.2.2
or there is a monomial
w
as given in one of Lemmas
3.4.3
,
3.4.4
such that
x=wz_{1}^{2^{u}}
with
u
nonnegative integers,
2\leqslant u\leqslant 4
, and
z_{1}
a monomial of weight vector
(3)|^{4-u}|(1)
. By Theorem
2.5
,
x
is inadmissible. This contradicts the fact that
x
is admissible. Hence,
x=g_{j}
for suitable
1\leqslant j\leqslant 930
.

Now we prove that the set
\{[g_{j}]_{\omega}:1\leqslant j\leqslant 915\}
is linearly independent in
QP_{5}(\omega)
.
Suppose there is a linear relation

\mathcal{S}:=\sum_{1\leqslant j\leqslant 930}\gamma_{j}g_{j}\equiv_{\omega}0,

(5.2)

where
\gamma_{j}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}
.

Let
u_{t},\,1\leqslant t\leqslant 35
, be as in Subsubsection
5.2.2
and the homomorphism
p_{(i;I)}:P_{5}\to P_{4}
which is defined by (
2.2
) for
k=5
. From Lemma
2.9
, we see that
p_{(i;I)}
passes to a homomorphism from
QP_{5}(\omega)
to
QP_{4}(\omega)
. For each
(i;I)\in\mathcal{N}_{5}
, we compute
p_{(i;I)}(\mathcal{S})
in terms of
w_{u},\,1\leqslant u\leqslant 35
(mod
P_{4}^{-}(\omega)+\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{4}
). By a direct computation we see that
p_{(i;I)}(\mathcal{S})\equiv_{\omega}0
for all
(i;I)\in\mathcal{N}_{5}
if and only if

\mathcal{S}=\sum_{331\leqslant j\leqslant 335}\gamma_{j}\tilde{\theta}_{j}+\sum_{916\leqslant j\leqslant 930}\gamma_{j}\tilde{\theta}_{j}\equiv_{\omega}0,

(5.3)

where the polynomials
\tilde{\theta}_{j}
are determined as in Subsubsection
5.2.3
. Hence, we get
\dim\widetilde{QP}_{5}(\omega)=1085
and

\widetilde{SF}_{5}(\omega)=\langle\{[\tilde{\theta}_{j}]_{\omega}:331\leqslant j\leqslant 335\mbox{ or }916\leqslant j\leqslant 930\}\rangle.

From
[
21
]
we see that
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{8}
is admissible. So, by using Proposition
2.11
, the leading monomials of the polynomials
\tilde{\theta}_{j}
,
331\leqslant j\leqslant 335
are admissible.
Hence,
5\leqslant\dim\widetilde{SF}_{5}(\omega)\leqslant 20
. The proposition is proved.
∎

Thus, we have the following result.

Theorem 5.2.3
.

There exist
1679
admissible monomials of degree
64
in
P_{5}
. Consequently
1679\leqslant\dim(QP_{5})_{64}\leqslant 1694.

5.3.
Computation of
(QP_{5})_{128}

By Lemma
3.1.1
, we have

(QP_{5})_{128}\cong QP_{5}((2)|(1)|^{6})\bigoplus QP_{5}((4)|(2)|^{5})\bigoplus QP_{5}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{3}|(1)).

By Theorems
3.2.1
and
3.3.1
, we have

\dim QP_{5}((2)|(1)|^{4})=124,\ \dim QP_{5}((4)|(2)|^{4})=465.

Hence, we need to compute
QP_{5}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{3}|(1))
.

5.3.1.
Computation of
QP_{5}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{3}|(1))

By using a result in
[
21
]
, we have
B_{4}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{3}|(1))=B_{4}^{+}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{3}|(1))
and
|B_{4}^{+}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{3}|(1))|=45
. This implies
\dim QP_{5}^{0}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}|(1))=5\times 45=225.
Hence, we need only to compute
QP_{5}^{+}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{2}|(1))
.
We prove the following result.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

i)
There exist exactly
1395
classes in
\widetilde{QP}_{5}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{3}|(1))
which are presented by
admissible monomials of degree
128
in
P_{5}
.

ii)

\dim\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{3}|(1))\leqslant 6
. Consequently,

1395\leqslant\dim\widetilde{QP}_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{3}|(1))\leqslant 1401.

Hence, the monomials
\tilde{w}_{t},\,1\leqslant t\leqslant 8
, are strictly inadmissible.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.3.1
.

Let
x\in P_{5}^{+}(\tilde{\omega})
be an admissible monomial with
\tilde{\omega}:=(4)|^{2}|(3)|^{3}|(1)
. Then
x=X_{r}y^{2}
with
1\leqslant r\leqslant 5
and
y
an admissible monomial of weight vector
(4)|(3)|^{3}|(1)
. By a direct computation we see that if
x\neq\tilde{g}_{j},\,1\leqslant j\leqslant 1176
, then either
x
is one of monomials as given in Lemma
5.3.2
or there is a monomial
w
as given in one of Lemmas
3.4.3
,
3.4.4
and
3.4.5
such that
x=wz_{1}^{2^{u}}
with
u
nonnegative integers,
2\leqslant u\leqslant 5
, and
z_{1}
a monomial of weight vector
(3)|^{6-u}
. By Theorem
2.5
,
x
is inadmissible. This contradicts the fact that
x
is admissible. Hence,
x=\tilde{g}_{j}
for some
j,\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant 1176
.

Now we prove that the set
\{[\tilde{g}_{j}]_{\tilde{\omega}}:1\leqslant j\leqslant 1176\}
is linearly independent in
QP_{5}(\tilde{\omega})
.

Suppose there is a linear relation

\tilde{\mathcal{S}}:=\sum_{1\leqslant j\leqslant 1176}\gamma_{j}\tilde{g}_{j}\equiv_{\tilde{\omega}}0,

(5.4)

where
\gamma_{j}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}
.
Let
\tilde{u}_{i},\,1\leqslant i\leqslant 45
, be as in Subsubsection
5.3.2
and the homomorphism
p_{(i;I)}:P_{5}\to P_{4}
which is defined by (
2.2
) for
k=5
. From Lemma
2.9
, we see that
p_{(i;I)}
passes to a homomorphism from
QP_{5}(\tilde{\omega})
to
QP_{4}(\tilde{\omega})
. By a routine computation we see that
p_{(i;I)}(\tilde{\mathcal{S}})\equiv_{\tilde{\omega}}0
for all
(i;I)\in\mathcal{N}_{5}
if and only if

\tilde{\mathcal{S}}=\sum_{1171\leqslant j\leqslant 1176}\gamma_{j}\eta_{j}\equiv_{\tilde{\omega}}0.

The leading monomial of
\eta_{j}
is
\tilde{g}_{j}
with
1171\leqslant j\leqslant 1176
.

The above facts imply that the monomials
\tilde{g}_{j}
with
1\leqslant j\leqslant 1170
are admissible,
\dim\widetilde{QP}_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{3}|(1))=1395
and
\dim\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|^{2}|(3)|^{3}|(1))\leqslant 6
. The proposition is proved.
∎

Combining the above results we obtain the following.

Corollary 5.3.3
.

We have

\dim\widetilde{QP}_{5}((4)|^{2}(3)|^{3}|(1))=1395\mbox{ and }1984\leqslant\dim(QP_{5})_{128}\leqslant 1990.
